### queue row OF-74

| OF-74 | **`--force-with-lease` is inert when the push target is a URL instead of a tracked remote, and this repository can only push by URL.** `grep -cin "force-with-lease" .kenovis/AI/policies/git.md` → **3**, mandated in all three ("Use `--force-with-lease`, never `--force`"). The lease compares against the *remote-tracking ref* for the branch. Pushing to an explicit HTTPS URL creates no such ref, so the lease has no baseline: here it exited `! [rejected] (stale info)` and refused a legitimate push. That is the safe direction. The unsafe one is the same mechanism with a stale tracking ref, where the lease passes against a baseline that is no longer what the remote holds — protection that reports success and checked the wrong thing. Reached by URL because `origin` is SSH with no key available (OF-65), which makes URL pushes this repository's only path rather than an unusual one. | This session, 2026-08-14, hit live | **Open.** Distinct from OF-65, checked before taking an id: OF-65 is `git fetch origin` exiting `128` with no instruction for the failure; this is a *different command* whose stated safety property silently does not hold under the workaround OF-65 forces. Cost is low and the fix is known because this session used it — `--force-with-lease=<ref>:<sha>` with the remote SHA read from the API first, which restores the guarantee explicitly instead of relying on a ref that does not exist. Priority: Pain high (a safety rule that is inert is worse than an absent one, because it is cited as the reason force-pushing is acceptable), Frequency: every force-push in an Installation whose remote the agent reaches by URL, Cost low. **Refined 2026-08-19 (DECISION-036's mechanism, lowest-id `Open` row untouched by any prior refinement pass)** — premise re-checked against the tree rather than carried over: `grep -cin "force-with-lease" framework/policies/git.md` still returns **3**, still bare (`--force-with-lease`, never `=<ref>:<sha>`), still no mention of the URL-push-inertness case this row names. Dimensions unchanged. Citation corrected: "item 42 part 2" is closed (2026-08-14, DECISION-034 among its outputs) and never touched this row's own gap — the two findings sit together only conceptually (both trace to `origin` being SSH-only, OF-65), not administratively; still Open, still paired with OF-65 for whoever picks either up. **Fixed (2026-08-21, via `/next`)** — `framework/policies/git.md` → "Use `--force-with-lease`, never `--force`" gains the URL-qualified form this row asked for: read the remote SHA with `git ls-remote` first, then push `--force-with-lease=<branch>:<remote-sha>` against the explicit HTTPS URL. Landed in the same change as OF-75, per that row's own "take it with OF-74" note. Closes [[Learning-035]] with it. |


### queue row OF-75

| OF-75 | **Nothing in the framework covers a stacked pull request, and the standard merge breaks one by construction.** `grep -cin "stacked\|dependent branch" .kenovis/AI/policies/git.md` → **0**. This session opened PR #111 from PR #110's head. Merging #110 with `gh pr merge --rebase` rewrote its commits onto `development`, and #111 immediately went `CONFLICTING/DIRTY` carrying **3 commits** — its own plus two whose content was already merged under different SHAs. `policies/git.md` → "Rebasing" tells you to rebase and resolve, which here means resolving conflicts between a branch and an already-merged copy of itself: the most error-prone form of the operation, on the one occasion when a wrong resolution silently drops merged work. | This session, 2026-08-14, hit live | **Open.** The recovery is the reusable part and is what makes this cheap rather than theoretical: capture the target tree hash **before** the base merges, rebuild the branch on the new base, and assert tree equality rather than reading the diff. Run here, and the assertion earned its keep on the first attempt — `git add -A` had staged `claude-info.md`, the untracked file OF-45b rejects committing, and the tree hash caught it where a diff review plausibly would not have. Recorded in [[Learning-035]]. Priority: Pain high when it fires (the failure mode is losing merged work while believing a conflict was resolved), Frequency low-medium (any round that opens a PR on an unmerged branch, which this session did once in five), Cost low — a `policies/git.md` section stating the sequence and the assertion. Take it with OF-74; both are `git.md` and both were found by the same merge. Role: cto (framework-layer fix, `framework/policies/git.md`). **Refined 2026-08-19 (DECISION-036, lowest-id `Open` row untouched by any prior refinement pass)** — premise re-checked against the tree rather than carried over: `grep -cin "stacked\|dependent branch" framework/policies/git.md` → **0**, unchanged (path corrected from the row's original `.kenovis/AI/policies/git.md` citation, stale since DECISION-039 moved framework source to `framework/`). Truth-value and priority both unchanged. **Fixed (2026-08-21, via `/next`)** — `framework/policies/git.md` gains a new "Stacked Pull Requests" section: capture the stacked branch's tree hash before the base merges, rebuild on the new base after leveling, assert tree equality rather than reading the diff, and do not `git add -A` during the rebuild. Cites [[Learning-035]] directly, including its `claude-info.md` staging instance. Landed in the same change as OF-74, as this row's own text asked. |

### queue row OF-69

| OF-69 | **A merge that lands on the remote and fails locally prints only the failure, so the session reads it as "the merge did not happen".** Reproduced on PR #107, 2026-08-13: `gh pr merge 107 --rebase --admin --delete-branch` output nothing but `fatal: Could not read from remote repository.` and `! warning: not possible to fast-forward to: "development"` — while `gh pr view 107` reported `MERGED` at `16:16:13Z` with merge commit `4ad5db6`. The remote half had succeeded. The local half — delete the feature branch, check out the base, fast-forward it — needed `origin` over SSH (OF-65) and died, after the checkout had already moved. Net state: working tree on a `development` **1 commit behind**, with the local feature branch gone and `git status -sb` printing a clean `## development...origin/development`, because the tracking ref was never updated either. | This session, 2026-08-13, reproduced live | **Fixed** (2026-08-18) — `policies/git.md` → "Rebasing" now states exactly this: confirm a merge from `gh pr view`'s own state rather than the command's exit, then repair the local checkout via `fetch` (HTTPS credential-helper) + `merge --ff-only` (OF-86). **Row corrected, not newly fixed**: two earlier rounds already asserted this closed — the "Fix OF-93" round's own commit message ("OF-69 closed via this round's Refine action") and OF-85's own "Ninth instance" note (this same document, below) — but neither round actually edited this row's disposition; it sat reading `**Open.**` through two further rounds, including one that picked a different row for its own Refine action without noticing this one's text had never caught up to its own history. Found and corrected here as this round's Refine action (DECISION-036), verified against `policies/git.md` directly rather than taken on citation (OF-30/Learning-031) — the fix that closes it was confirmed live, twice, on PRs #143 and #144, per that same paragraph. Distinct from the two findings it sits between, checked against both before taking an id: OF-60 is `/bootstrap` not verifying currency at session start, OF-65 is the `git fetch origin` instruction having no branch for exit `128`. Neither covers a **composite command whose halves have different outcomes and whose output reports one of them**. The danger is specific and not hypothetical — a session that believes the merge failed will retry it, re-open the PR, or rebuild the branch, and every one of those acts on a repository where the work already landed. Priority: Pain high (a wrong belief about whether a merge happened is upstream of every recovery action a round would take), Frequency: every merge in an Installation whose remote the agent cannot reach over the configured transport, Cost low — the rule is to confirm the merge from the *PR's* state rather than the command's exit, then repair the local checkout, which is two commands. Belongs with OF-65 in item 42 part 2, which is already editing session-start git behaviour; the two are one clause about not trusting a git command's own report over the artifact's state. **Second instance, same day, on the PR that queued this row:** `gh pr merge 108 --rebase --admin --delete-branch` produced the identical output and `gh pr view 108` returned `MERGED 2026-08-13T16:19:38Z 0d539c5`. Two for two, so the Frequency term above is measured rather than predicted, and the repair is confirmed as two commands (`fetch` over HTTPS with an explicit refspec, then `merge --ff-only`). **Further instances are not individually recordable and this row is closed to them** — each merge here reproduces it by construction, so appending one line per merge would grow the document this queue is already over threshold on (OF-23, OF-62) while adding nothing to the disposition. Not compacted here, same reasoning OF-85's own "Ninth instance" already gave for this exact row: a manual archive-move is scope beyond a routine Refine action and is left for a dedicated archive-pass round. |

### item 41

**The conformance table has moved to `PRODUCT/OPERATING_MODEL.md` → "Conformance"** (2026-08-14, step 2 below, DECISION-032). ### Round narrative — OF-38/OF-28/OF-29 fixed (2026-08-14, via `/next`)

OF-38, OF-28 & OF-29, 2026-08-14, via /next — item 41 step 4 begins: the disposition rule fires at an event that occurs, and reaches the one window it did not.

Premises checked before scoping, per Learning-023: `grep -n "before the session ends" CLAUDE.md .kenovis/AI/SYSTEM.md .kenovis/AI/policies/documentation.md` → three hits, confirming OF-38's row. `grep -n "PRODUCT/OPERATING_MODEL.md.*section\|What the AI-OS owns" .kenovis/AI/templates/product-layer/PRODUCT/OPERATING_MODEL.md` checked before citing a section number in the fix, after OF-58's own near-miss on the same mistake last round — this repository's founder-authored document numbers its sections differently from the generic template every other Installation gets, so the fix cites no section number at all, only the commands.

**OF-38:** `CLAUDE.md` **2.6 → 2.7**, `.kenovis/AI/SYSTEM.md` **1.10 → 1.11**, `policies/documentation.md` **3.8 → 3.9**. All three re-anchor the disposition rule's trigger from "before the session ends" — an event nothing detects, since a thread simply stops — to the moment of discovery, and name the one real exception: the window before `init-project.md`/`adopt-project.md` has authored the Product layer, where OF-28 and OF-29 apply.

**OF-28:** `cli/src/domain/installation.ts` → `claudeStubContent`. The pending stub (written between `kenovis init`/`kenovis add` and the end of setup) unconditionally listed `PRODUCT/ROADMAP.md`, `DECISIONS.md` and three more destinations that `runInit` never creates (DECISION-021) — so the one file Claude Code autoloads told a pre-setup session to route findings to paths that did not exist. It now says so, and names the setup command as where they go until it creates the real destinations. Two new unit tests in `installation.test.ts`; a pre-existing test in `init.test.ts` asserted the bug (`stub names PRODUCT/ROADMAP.md as a destination`, on a stub `runInit` always leaves pending) and is corrected to assert the fix instead.

**OF-29:** `.kenovis/AI/commands/init-project.md` **1.11 → 1.12** gains Step 13, `adopt-project.md` **1.11 → 1.12** gains Step 14 — both placed after the existing final step rather than renumbering it, since that step is cited by id from `learnings.md`, DECISION-018 and DECISION-022 (the same blast-radius reasoning item 41 step 2 already applied once). Each closes the run with a disposition pass now that every destination it authored actually exists. `adopt-project.md`'s version is the one this framework expects to matter — it is the session that reads an entire pre-existing codebase end to end, once, and item 41 step 4 already ranks its class of finding as the highest-yield unscheduled work on this board (OF-02/OF-03's precedent).

Validation, run rather than reasoned about. `grep -n "before the session ends"` → 0 across the three files. `cd cli && npm run typecheck && npm test` → typecheck clean, **112/112** tests pass (up from 111/112 before the `init.test.ts` correction). `grep -n "^# Step 12 - Record The Initialization" init-project.md` and `"^# Step 13 - Record The Adoption" adopt-project.md` → both present, unchanged — the four external citations to those steps still resolve.

**Operating model section served: §2** — *"ABSOLUTE PRIORITY #1: nothing discovered may be lost... Whenever Kenovis discovers something... it MUST be planned. There are NO exceptions."* **The row does not move; it stays `Partial`**, on the same discipline OF-30 and Learning-031 require: the design is now correct by construction (unconditional load, a trigger that fires, a named pre-setup path), but nothing has run `/init-project` or `/adopt-project` end to end since Step 13/14 landed, so the behavioural half — does a real setup session actually catch something and disposition it — is asserted by the round that authored it and is not evidence. That run is item 41 step 4's own next natural validation point, not a new finding.

### Round narrative — OF-31/OF-55 fixed (2026-08-14, via `/next`)

OF-31 & OF-55, 2026-08-14, via /next — item 41 §12: findings route by role, and this repository names its own.

Premise checked before scoping, per Learning-023: `grep -rn "Open Findings\|disposition" .kenovis/AI/agents/*.md` → **0** across twelve files, confirming OF-31's row. `grep -n "framework\|policy\|markdown" .kenovis/AI/agents/*.md` → **3** hits, confirming OF-55's row.

Caught mid-draft, before it shipped: the first version of this fix named `.kenovis/AI/agents/cto.md` as OF-55's destination and `.kenovis/AI/` as an example inside the framework-layer disposition rule. Both are wrong the moment this text reaches a second Installation — `cto.md` is never rewritten per product, and "you own `.kenovis/AI/`" is true of this repository specifically (DECISION-020's self-modification exception) and false of every customer Installation, where `.kenovis/AI/` is a read-only mirror no role ever touches. Corrected before writing, not after — the same class of near-miss OF-58's own round caught in itself the round before this one, on the same document.

**DECISION-035** carries the full reasoning and four rejected alternatives. Two changes, split exactly where the mistake above says they must be:

`.kenovis/AI/policies/documentation.md` **3.9 → 3.10** — an `Open` finding names the role that owns analysing it, from the existing Agent Roster, as a fourth dimension beside Pain/Frequency/Cost. Which role owns which *kind* of finding is stated nowhere in this framework-layer rule — that fact belongs to each product's own roster and its own maintained areas.

`ENGINEERING/ARCHITECTURE.md` **1.6 → 1.7** — this repository's own instance: the CTO role owns findings whose destination is `.kenovis/AI/`, recorded beside the CI-guards section that already states a this-repository-only fact in the same voice.

**The roster question OF-31 also asked — are `ceo`/`marketing`/`finance`/`legal` a gap or a deletion — is answered, not deferred:** a gap, not a deletion. `COMPANY_OS.md` already names Marketing, Finance and Legal analysis as areas AI agents support, and `PRODUCT/ROADMAP.md` → Product Strategy places that work in Phase 2 and beyond. Dormant by product stage, unlike `workflows/roadmap.md` (OF-81), which had zero conceptual justification and a superseding replacement already four rounds old.

Validation, run rather than reasoned about. `grep -n "cto.md" .kenovis/AI/policies/documentation.md` → **0** — the corrected draft cites no framework-layer agent file by name. `grep -c "^| 2 |\|ownership" ENGINEERING/ARCHITECTURE.md` confirms the new section landed once, not duplicated. `check_decision_index.py` → 28 decision bodies, 28 index lines. Ten CI guards pass.

**Operating model section served: §12** — *"The role that owns the responsibility must process the discovery."* **The row does not move; it stays `Absent`.** What changed: a finding can now be tagged with an owning role, and this repository's own framework-layer ownership is named — but nothing routes an active session to the tagged role, no agent file reads its own tag, and the twelve roster files still carry zero findings-awareness of their own. Naming an owner is not the same as the owner processing anything; OF-32 (refinement) is the very next step in this same sequence and is still fully open.

Findings this round did not fix: none.
